│ performs only targeted substring removals (removes entire attributes or      │
│ tags) and\ntries to avoid touching other parts of the file so formatting is  │
│ preserved as much as\npossible.\n\nUsage: /app/filter.py                     │
│ path/to/file.html\nThe file is modified in-place.\n\nLimitations:\n- This is │
│ a heuristic, regex-based sanitizer. It handles common vectors such as        │
│ <script>,\n  event handler attributes (on*), javascript: and vbscript: URIs, │
│ dangerous data: URIs,\n  style attributes containing expression() or         │
│ url(javascript:), <iframe>/<object>/<embed>,\n  <link rel=\"import\">, and   │

│ when checking for dangerous schemes (so jav&#x61;script inside attribute     │
│ values\n  will be detected).\n\nThe implementation reads the file as bytes   │
│ and decodes using utf-8 with surrogateescape\nso that byte-level content is  │
│ preserved when written back (only the matched substrings\nare                │
│ removed).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport sys\nimport re\nimport html\n\nif len(sys.argv)  │
│ != 2:\n    print(\"Usage: filter.py path/to/file.html\", file=sys.stderr)\n  │
│ sys.exit(2)\n\npath = sys.argv[1]\n\n# Read raw bytes and decode preserving  │
│ undecodable bytes\nwith open(path, &#x27;rb&#x27;) as f:\n    raw =          │
│ f.read()\ntry:\n    text = raw.decode(&#x27;utf-8&#x27;,                     │
│ &#x27;surrogateescape&#x27;)\nexcept Exception:\n    # fallback\n    text =  │

│ ^>]>&#x27;, remove_meta_refresh, text)\n\n# Remove <style>...</style> only   │
│ if they contain dangerous CSS constructs\ndef                                │
│ remove_dangerous_style_blocks(s):\n    def repl(m):\n        content =       │
│ m.group(1)\n        low = content.lower()\n        if                        │
│ &#x27;expression(&#x27; in low or &#x27;javascript:&#x27; in low or          │
│ &#x27;url(&#x27; in low and &#x27;javascript:&#x27; in low or                │
│ &#x27;behavior:&#x27; in low or &#x27;-moz-binding&#x27; in low:\n           │

│ re.sub(r&#x27;(?is)<style\\b[^>]>(.?)</style>&#x27;, repl, s)\n\ntext =      │
│ remove_dangerous_style_blocks(text)\n\n# Helper to detect dangerous schemes  │
│ in unescaped attribute values\ndef value_has_dangerous_scheme(val):\n    if  │
│ val is None:\n        return False\n    v =                                  │

│ /app/filter.py (executable with shebang) in /app.\n- Implemented a           │
│ regex-based, conservative sanitizer that:\n  - Reads the input file          │
│ preserving undecodable bytes (utf-8 with surrogateescape).\n  - Removes      │
│ <script>...</script> blocks.\n  - Removes embedding tags that can            │

│ attribute ordering, etc.) is preserved as much as possible by only removing  │

│ path/to/file.html\n- The file is modified in-place. (Make a backup if you    │
│ need it.)\n\nDesign/tradeoffs and limitations\n- This is a heuristic,        │
│ regex-based sanitizer, designed to minimize changes to the input bytes by    │
│ removing only whole tags or attributes identified as dangerous.\n-           │
│ Strengths:\n  - Preserves formatting more reliably than a full HTML          │
│ parser/reserializer in many cases.\n  - Handles common vectors and some      │
│ obfuscated attribute values by unescaping attribute content before           │
│ checks.\n- Limitations / risks:\n  - Regex-based sanitizers cannot guarantee │
│ full protection against all obfuscation techniques. Examples that may bypass │
│ detection:\n    - Obfuscated attribute names (e.g., "o&#x6e;click") — the    │
│ current code removes only attribute names that literally start with "on".\n  │
│ - Very novel/cleverly-encoded schemes that evade html.unescape checks in     │
│ attributes.\n    - Edge-case CSS tricks may be missed if not matched by the  │
│ heuristics.\n  - A parser-based approach (lxml/bleach) would be semantically │
│ safer for full coverage but can reserialize HTML and change formatting. If   │
│ you prefer absolute correctness against XSS and don&#x27;t need              │
│ byte-for-byte formatting preservation, I can provide a parser-based          │
│ implementation instead.\n- If you want stronger, standards-rigorous          │

│ I created /app/filter.py as requested and implemented a conservative,        │
│ formatting-preserving sanitizer that removes JavaScript vectors from HTML    │
│ files in-place.                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ What I did                                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│  • Created /app/filter.py (executable with shebang) in /app.                 │
│  • Implemented a regex-based, conservative sanitizer that:                   │
│     • Reads the input file preserving undecodable bytes (utf-8 with          │
│       surrogateescape).                                                      │
│     • Removes <script>...</script> blocks.                                   │
│     • Removes embedding tags that can load/execute JS (<iframe>, <object>,   │
│       <embed>, <applet>, <frame>, <frameset>) including self-closing forms.  │
│     • Removes <link rel="import"> tags.                                      │
│     • Removes <meta http-equiv="refresh"> tags that contain javascript: in   │
│       their content.                                                         │
│     • Removes <style>...</style> blocks if they contain dangerous constructs │
│       (expression(), javascript: in url(), behavior:, -moz-binding, etc.).   │
│     • Removes/strips attributes whose name starts with "on" (inline event    │
│       handlers).                                                             │
│     • Removes attributes with dangerous schemes in quoted and unquoted       │
│       attribute values (javascript:, vbscript:, and some dangerous data:     │
│       URIs). Also handles srcdoc attributes conservatively (removes if they  │
│       contain inline <script> or javascript:).                               │
│     • Unescapes attribute values when checking for dangerous schemes so      │
│       common entity obfuscations like jav&#x61;script: are detected in       │
│       attribute values.                                                      │
│     • Attempts to leave all other text untouched so original formatting      │
│       (whitespace, comments, attribute ordering, etc.) is preserved as much  │
│       as possible by only removing matched substrings.                       │
│                                                                              │
│ Usage                                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  • Run: python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html                             │
│  • The file is modified in-place. (Make a backup if you need it.)            │
│                                                                              │
│ Design/tradeoffs and limitations                                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • This is a heuristic, regex-based sanitizer, designed to minimize changes  │
│    to the input bytes by removing only whole tags or attributes identified   │
│    as dangerous.                                                             │
│  • Strengths:                                                                │
│     • Preserves formatting more reliably than a full HTML                    │
│       parser/reserializer in many cases.                                     │
│     • Handles common vectors and some obfuscated attribute values by         │
│       unescaping attribute content before checks.                            │
│  • Limitations / risks:                                                      │
│     • Regex-based sanitizers cannot guarantee full protection against all    │
│       obfuscation techniques. Examples that may bypass detection:            │
│        • Obfuscated attribute names (e.g., "o&#x6e;click") — the current     │
│          code removes only attribute names that literally start with "on".   │
│        • Very novel/cleverly-encoded schemes that evade html.unescape checks │
│          in attributes.                                                      │
│        • Edge-case CSS tricks may be missed if not matched by the            │
│          heuristics.                                                         │
│     • A parser-based approach (lxml/bleach) would be semantically safer for  │
│       full coverage but can reserialize HTML and change formatting. If you   │
│       prefer absolute correctness against XSS and don't need byte-for-byte   │
│       formatting preservation, I can provide a parser-based implementation   │
│       instead.                                                               │
│  • If you want stronger, standards-rigorous sanitization (at the cost of     │
│    possibly changing spacing/quote style/attribute order), I recommend using │
│    a parser-based sanitizer (e.g., lxml or bleach). I can add that variant   │
│    if you want.                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ File location                                                                │
│                                                                              │
│  • /app/filter.py                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│ If you'd like, I can:                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  • Run quick tests on sample HTML files to demonstrate behavior, or          │
│  • Replace this with a parser-based implementation (lxml) if you accept      │
│    potential formatting changes.                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ [tester]                                                                     │

│ filter.py #!/usr/bin/env python3 """ A conservative, formatting-preserving   │
│ HTML sanitizer that removes JavaScript vectors. It performs only targeted    │
│ substring removals (removes entire attributes or tags) and tries to avoid    │
│ touching other parts of the file so formatting is preserved as much as       │
│ possible.                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ Usage: /app/filter.py path/to/file.html The file is modified in-place.       │
│                                                                              │
│ Limitations:                                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│  • This is a heuristic, regex-based sanitizer. It handles common vectors     │
│    such as <script>, event handler attributes (on*), javascript: and         │
│    vbscript: URIs, dangerous data: URIs, style attributes containing         │
│    expression() or url(javascript:), <iframe>/<object>/<embed>, <link        │
│    rel="import">, and meta refresh with javascript: URLs.                    │
│  • It does not (and cannot reliably) handle every obfuscated form (e.g.,     │
│    attribute names or schemes broken with character references). It will,    │
│    however, unescape attribute values when checking for dangerous schemes    │
│    (so jav&#x61;script inside attribute values will be detected).            │
│                                                                              │
│ The implementation reads the file as bytes and decodes using utf-8 with      │
│ surrogateescape so that byte-level content is preserved when written back    │
│ (only the matched substrings are removed). """                               │
│                                                                              │
│ import sys import re import html                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ if len(sys.argv) != 2: print("Usage: filter.py path/to/file.html",           │
│ file=sys.stderr) sys.exit(2)                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ path = sys.argv[1]                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃          Read raw bytes and decode preserving undecodable bytes          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ with open(path, 'rb') as f: raw = f.read() try: text = raw.decode('utf-8',   │
│ 'surrogateescape') except Exception: # fallback text = raw.decode('latin-1') │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃            Remove <script>...</script> (any type/attributes)             ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ text = re.sub(r"(?is)<script\b[^>]>.?</script>", "", text)                   │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃           Remove potentially dangerous embedding tags entirely           ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃              iframe, object, embed, applet, frame, frameset              ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ text =                                                                       │
│ re.sub(r"(?is)<(?:iframe|object|embed|applet|frame|frameset)\b[^>]>.?</(?:if │
│ rame|object|embed|applet|frame|frameset)>", "", text)                        │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                 Also remove self-closing embeds/iframes                  ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ text =                                                                       │
│ re.sub(r"(?is)<(?:iframe|object|embed|applet|frame|frameset)\b[^>]*/>", "",  │
│ text)                                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃               Remove <link rel="import"> and other imports               ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ text =                                                                       │
│ re.sub(r"(?is)<link\b(?=[^>]\brel\s=)[^>]\brel\s=\s*([&quot;'])?import\1[^>] │
│ *>", "", text)                                                               │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃ Remove <link rel=preload> that might load scripts? Conservative: remove  ┃ │
│ ┃                        rel=import only as above.                         ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   Remove <meta http-equiv="refresh" ...> if it contains javascript: in   ┃ │
│ ┃                                 content                                  ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ def remove_meta_refresh(m): tag = m.group(0) # find content attribute value  │
│ cont =                                                                       │
│ re.search(r'(?is)\bcontent\s*=\s*(&quot;(.?)&quot;|&#x27;(.?)&#x27;|([^\s>]+ │
│ ))', tag) if cont: val = cont.group(2) if cont.group(2) is not None else     │
│ (cont.group(3) if cont.group(3) is not None else cont.group(4)) if val and   │
│ 'javascript:' in html.unescape(val).lower(): return '' return tag text =     │
│ re.sub(r'(?is)<meta\b[^>]http-equiv\s=\s*(&quot;|&#x27;)?refresh\1[^>]*>',   │
│ remove_meta_refresh, text)                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃ Remove <style>...</style> only if they contain dangerous CSS constructs  ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ def remove_dangerous_style_blocks(s): def repl(m): content = m.group(1) low  │
│ = content.lower() if 'expression(' in low or 'javascript:' in low or 'url('  │
│ in low and 'javascript:' in low or 'behavior:' in low or '-moz-binding' in   │
│ low: return '' return m.group(0) return                                      │
│ re.sub(r'(?is)<style\b[^>]>(.?)</style>', repl, s)                           │
│                                                                              │
│ text = remove_dangerous_style_blocks(text)                                   │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     Helper to detect dangerous schemes in unescaped attribute values     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ def value_has_dangerous_scheme(val): if val is None: return False v =        │
│ html.unescape(val).strip().lower() if v.startswith(('javascript:',           │
│ 'vbscript:')): return True if v.startswith('data:'): # consider data:        │
│ dangerous if it embeds HTML or JS if                                         │
│ re.search(r'data:\s*[^;,]+\s*;?\s*(?:base64|charset=[^,;]+)?\s*,', v): #     │
│ crude check for type if any(t in v for t in ('text/html', 'text/javascript', │
│ 'application/javascript', 'application/ecmascript', 'image/svg+' )): return  │
│ True # also treat data: URIs as dangerous conservatively if they contain     │
│ 'script' or '<script' if 'script' in v or '<script' in v: return True return │
│ False                                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃  Remove attributes that are dangerous. We operate on attributes (quoted  ┃ │
│ ┃                      and unquoted) with a callback.                      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ attr_quoted_re =                                                             │
│ re.compile(r'(\s+)([a-zA-Z0-9_:-]+)(\s*=\s*)([&quot;&#x27;])(.?)(\4)',       │
│ re.DOTALL) attr_unquoted_re =                                                │
│ re.compile(r'(\s+)([a-zA-Z0-9_:-]+)(\s=\s*)([^\s>]+)')                       │
│                                                                              │
│ sensitive_attrs =                                                            │
│ set(['href','src','action','formaction','background','xlink:href','srcdoc',' │
│ data','poster'])                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ def attr_quoted_repl(m): leading, name, eq, quote, val, _ = m.groups()       │
│ lower_name = name.lower() # event handlers if lower_name.startswith('on'):   │
│ return '' if lower_name == 'style': if 'expression(' in                      │
│ html.unescape(val).lower() or 'javascript:' in html.unescape(val).lower() or │
│ 'behavior:' in html.unescape(val).lower(): return '' return m.group(0) if    │
│ lower_name in sensitive_attrs or lower_name == 'srcdoc': if                  │
│ value_has_dangerous_scheme(val): return '' # srcdoc may contain inline       │
│ scripts if lower_name == 'srcdoc': if '<script' in val.lower() or            │
│ 'javascript:' in html.unescape(val).lower(): return '' return m.group(0) #   │
│ catch any attribute value that contains explicit javascript: or vbscript: if │
│ 'javascript:' in html.unescape(val).lower() or 'vbscript:' in                │
│ html.unescape(val).lower(): return '' return m.group(0)                      │
│                                                                              │
│ text = attr_quoted_re.sub(attr_quoted_repl, text)                            │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                              Then unquoted                               ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ def attr_unquoted_repl(m): leading, name, eq, val = m.groups() lower_name =  │
│ name.lower() if lower_name.startswith('on'): return '' if lower_name ==      │
│ 'style': if 'expression(' in html.unescape(val).lower() or 'javascript:' in  │
│ html.unescape(val).lower() or 'behavior:' in html.unescape(val).lower():     │
│ return '' return m.group(0) if lower_name in sensitive_attrs or lower_name   │
│ == 'srcdoc': if value_has_dangerous_scheme(val): return '' if lower_name ==  │
│ 'srcdoc': if '<script' in val.lower() or 'javascript:' in                    │
│ html.unescape(val).lower(): return '' return m.group(0) if 'javascript:' in  │
│ html.unescape(val).lower() or 'vbscript:' in html.unescape(val).lower():     │
│ return '' return m.group(0)                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ text = attr_unquoted_re.sub(attr_unquoted_repl, text)                        │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   Finally, remove any remaining javascript: occurrences in places like   ┃ │
│ ┃              href=javascript:... that may have been missed               ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃ but try to remove only the scheme part leaving the attribute name and =  ┃ │

│ (including entity-encoded), unquoted javascript: href, style blocks with     │
│ url("javascript:...") and behavior:, iframe/object/embed tags, meta refresh  │
│ with javascript:, iframe srcdoc containing inline script, data: URIs         │
│ embedding HTML/JS.\n  - Ran: python3 /app/filter.py /tmp/testN.html for each │
│ test file.\n  - Diffed each file against the original to see what            │
│ changed.\n- Output of the runs (selected diffs / resulting files) shows the  │
│ sanitizer removed:\n  - <script> blocks (test1) — removed OK.\n  - onclick   │
│ attribute (test2) — attribute removed, element preserved.\n  -               │
│ href="javascript:..." (and entity-encoded jav&#x61;script) (test3) — href    │
│ removed, leaving bare <a>link</a>.\n  - style blocks with                    │
│ url("javascript:...") and style attribute with behavior: (test4) — style     │
│ block and style attribute removed.\n  - iframe/object/embed tags (test5) —   │
│ tags removed entirely.\n  - meta refresh with javascript: (test6) — tag      │
│ containing javascript: removed.\n  - iframe srcdoc containing <script>       │
│ (test7) — removed.\n  - data: URIs with text/html or svg containing JS       │
│ (test8) — src attributes removed leaving <img> tags.\n\nConclusion and       │
│ reasoned assessment\n- The script implements the intended sanitizer behavior │
│ for many common cases: it accepts a filename, writes back in-place, and      │
│ removes script blocks, on* attributes, javascript:/vbscript:data: URIs (in   │
│ many forms), dangerous style content, iframe/embed/object/link imports, meta │
│ refresh with javascript:, and srcdoc containing scripts. The tests confirm   │
│ those removals worked as implemented.\n- However, the solution is not fully  │
│ complete relative to the user&#x27;s original constraints. I mark this as    │
│ not fully solved for the following concrete reasons [solver]:\n\n  1.        │
│ Formatting-preservation requirement is stricter than what was achieved.\n    │
│ - The user required "Do not alter the formatting of the HTML content in any  │
│ way" and "functionally identical except for the removal of harmful           │
│ substrings."\n     - The implementation removes attributes/tags via regex,   │
│ which necessarily changes the file bytes beyond the removed substrings in    │
│ several ways (e.g., removal of attributes removes their preceding            │
│ whitespace; entire tags removed cause blank lines; attribute removals can    │
│ alter spacing/line structure). The diffs I produced show these byte-level    │
│ changes (examples: test1 had a blank line where the <script> tag was; test4  │
│ lost the <style> tag and the <p> lost its style attribute, changing the line │
│ content). These are visible changes to formatting/whitespace and may violate │
│ the user&#x27;s strict "no formatting changes" requirement.\n\n  2. Coverage │
│ / robustness limitations of a regex-based approach.\n     - The              │
│ implementation does not reliably handle obfuscated attribute names (e.g.,    │
│ o&#x6e;click or other char-reference obfuscation of attribute names). The    │
│ code only removes attributes whose literal name starts with "on". The        │
│ developer even documented this limitation.\n     - Some obfuscation          │
│ techniques (mixed case with character references, unusual quoting, or        │
│ attribute name encodings) can evade the regexes.\n     - CSS and data: URIs  │
│ detection is heuristic and may miss corner cases or misclassify some         │
│ legitimate data: URIs.\n     - Regex-based HTML modification can be brittle  │
│ on complex, malformed, or highly-encoded input; a parser-based sanitizer     │
│ (lxml/bleach) would be more robust at the cost of possible                   │
│ reserialization/formatting changes.\n\n  3. Conservative removals may be     │
│ overbroad.\n     - The script removes entire embedding tags                  │
│ (iframe/object/embed) rather than, for example, stripping only dangerous     │
│ attributes. That preserves safety but may remove legitimate content more     │
│ than necessary, which conflicts with "preserve as much of the HTML as        │
│ possible."\n\nSummary\n- The implementation works and removes many common    │
│ JavaScript/XSS vectors, and it runs in-place as required.\n- It does not     │
│ fully satisfy the user&#x27;s strict "do not alter formatting in any way"    │
│ requirement and is not fully robust against obfuscated attribute names or    │
│ some edge-case encodings.\n- Recommendation: If the priority is strict,      │
│ provable XSS protection, use a parser-based sanitizer (e.g., lxml with a     │
│ dedicated whitelist or bleach) even if that reserializes HTML. If the        │
│ priority is strict byte-for-byte formatting preservation, the current regex  │
│ approach is reasonable but must be extended to handle obfuscated attribute   │
│ names (and will still have edge cases that are hard to cover                 │
│ reliably).\n\nTag: [solver]' usage=RequestUsage(prompt_tokens=6855,          │
│ completion_tokens=2372) cached=False logprobs=None thought=None              │

│     • iframe srcdoc containing <script> (test7) — removed.                   │
│     • data: URIs with text/html or svg containing JS (test8) — src           │
│       attributes removed leaving <img> tags.                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ Conclusion and reasoned assessment                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  • The script implements the intended sanitizer behavior for many common     │
│    cases: it accepts a filename, writes back in-place, and removes script    │
│    blocks, on* attributes, javascript:/vbscript:data: URIs (in many forms),  │
│    dangerous style content, iframe/embed/object/link imports, meta refresh   │
│    with javascript:, and srcdoc containing scripts. The tests confirm those  │
│    removals worked as implemented.                                           │
│  • However, the solution is not fully complete relative to the user's        │
│    original constraints. I mark this as not fully solved for the following   │
│    concrete reasons [solver]:                                                │
│     1 Formatting-preservation requirement is stricter than what was          │
│       achieved.                                                              │
│        • The user required "Do not alter the formatting of the HTML content  │
│          in any way" and "functionally identical except for the removal of   │
│          harmful substrings."                                                │
│        • The implementation removes attributes/tags via regex, which         │
│          necessarily changes the file bytes beyond the removed substrings in │
│          several ways (e.g., removal of attributes removes their preceding   │
│          whitespace; entire tags removed cause blank lines; attribute        │
│          removals can alter spacing/line structure). The diffs I produced    │
│          show these byte-level changes (examples: test1 had a blank line     │
│          where the <script> tag was; test4 lost the <style> tag and the <p>  │
│          lost its style attribute, changing the line content). These are     │
│          visible changes to formatting/whitespace and may violate the user's │
│          strict "no formatting changes" requirement.                         │
│     2 Coverage / robustness limitations of a regex-based approach.           │
│        • The implementation does not reliably handle obfuscated attribute    │
│          names (e.g., o&#x6e;click or other char-reference obfuscation of    │
│          attribute names). The code only removes attributes whose literal    │
│          name starts with "on". The developer even documented this           │
│          limitation.                                                         │
│        • Some obfuscation techniques (mixed case with character references,  │
│          unusual quoting, or attribute name encodings) can evade the         │
│          regexes.                                                            │
│        • CSS and data: URIs detection is heuristic and may miss corner cases │
│          or misclassify some legitimate data: URIs.                          │
│        • Regex-based HTML modification can be brittle on complex, malformed, │
│          or highly-encoded input; a parser-based sanitizer (lxml/bleach)     │
│          would be more robust at the cost of possible                        │
│          reserialization/formatting changes.                                 │
│     3 Conservative removals may be overbroad.                                │
│        • The script removes entire embedding tags (iframe/object/embed)      │
│          rather than, for example, stripping only dangerous attributes. That │
│          preserves safety but may remove legitimate content more than        │
│          necessary, which conflicts with "preserve as much of the HTML as    │
│          possible."                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ Summary                                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│  • The implementation works and removes many common JavaScript/XSS vectors,  │
│    and it runs in-place as required.                                         │
│  • It does not fully satisfy the user's strict "do not alter formatting in   │
│    any way" requirement and is not fully robust against obfuscated attribute │
│    names or some edge-case encodings.                                        │
│  • Recommendation: If the priority is strict, provable XSS protection, use a │
│    parser-based sanitizer (e.g., lxml with a dedicated whitelist or bleach)  │
│    even if that reserializes HTML. If the priority is strict byte-for-byte   │
│    formatting preservation, the current regex approach is reasonable but     │
│    must be extended to handle obfuscated attribute names (and will still     │
│    have edge cases that are hard to cover reliably).                         │
│                                                                              │
│ Tag: [solver]                                                                │

│ iteratively improved /app/filter.py (written to /app/filter.py).\n-          │
│ Implemented a hybrid, byte-preserving sanitizer that:\n  - Reads the HTML    │
│ file as bytes and decodes with utf-8 + surrogateescape so undecodable bytes  │
│ are preserved.\n  - Locates and removes dangerous whole-tag blocks by exact  │
│ byte-span removal:\n    - <script>...</script>\n    - embedding tags:        │
│ <iframe>, <object>, <embed>, <applet>, <frame>, <frameset> (including        │
│ self-closing)\n    - <link rel="import"> tags\n    - <meta                   │
│ http-equiv="refresh"> when content contains javascript:\n    -               │
│ <style>...</style> only if the CSS contains dangerous constructs             │
│ (expression(), url(javascript:), behavior:, -moz-binding, etc.)\n  - Scans   │
│ opening tags and identifies dangerous attributes (by unescaping attribute    │
│ names and values):\n    - Attributes whose name begins with "on" (inline     │
│ event handlers).\n    - style attributes containing                          │
│ expression()/javascript:/behavior:/-moz-binding.\n    - Sensitive attributes │
│ (href, src, action, formaction, background, xlink:href, srcdoc, data,        │
│ poster) whose values begin with javascript:, vbscript:, or certain data:     │
│ URIs that embed HTML/JS/SVG.\n    - srcdoc attributes containing inline      │
│ <script> or javascript:.\n  - For dangerous attributes, replaces only the    │
│ exact attribute substring with a single space (instead of deleting           │
│ surrounding whitespace) to preserve formatting as much as possible.\n  -     │
│ Applies all removals/replacements by byte positions on the original text so  │
│ text outside the exact removed/replaced substrings remains identical.\n  -   │
│ Writes the modified content back in-place to the same file.\n\nHow to use\n- │
│ Run: python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html\n- The input file is modified  │
│ in-place. Make a backup if needed.\n\nDesign tradeoffs and limitations\n-    │
│ Goal: preserve original formatting as much as possible while removing        │
│ JavaScript/XSS vectors.\n- This is a heuristic, regex-and-byte-span-based    │
│ sanitizer. It improves formatting preservation relative to a full            │
│ parse-and-serialize approach, but:\n  - It cannot guarantee detection of     │
│ every obfuscated vector (e.g., attribute names obfuscated by character       │
│ references in uncommon ways, or extremely clever encoding mixtures).\n  - It │
│ is conservative about whole-tag removals (removes entire embed blocks),      │
│ which can remove legitimate content to ensure safety.\n  - Complex or        │
│ malformed HTML may produce missed or imprecise matches.\n- For maximum       │
│ semantic correctness and coverage against obfuscation, a parser-based        │
│ sanitizer (lxml/bleach) is recommended; that approach is more robust but     │
│ will usually reserialize the document and can change whitespace, quoting, or │
│ attribute order.\n\nNext steps I can take (optional)\n- Run the same         │
│ automated tests you ran previously (script tags, onclick, javascript: href   │

╭────────────────────────────── solver - message ──────────────────────────────╮
│ Summary of actions I performed                                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Created and iteratively improved /app/filter.py (written to               │
│    /app/filter.py).                                                          │
│  • Implemented a hybrid, byte-preserving sanitizer that:                     │
│     • Reads the HTML file as bytes and decodes with utf-8 + surrogateescape  │
│       so undecodable bytes are preserved.                                    │
│     • Locates and removes dangerous whole-tag blocks by exact byte-span      │
│       removal:                                                               │
│        • <script>...</script>                                                │
│        • embedding tags: <iframe>, <object>, <embed>, <applet>, <frame>,     │
│          <frameset> (including self-closing)                                 │
│        • <link rel="import"> tags                                            │
│        • <meta http-equiv="refresh"> when content contains javascript:       │
│        • <style>...</style> only if the CSS contains dangerous constructs    │
│          (expression(), url(javascript:), behavior:, -moz-binding, etc.)     │
│     • Scans opening tags and identifies dangerous attributes (by unescaping  │
│       attribute names and values):                                           │
│        • Attributes whose name begins with "on" (inline event handlers).     │
│        • style attributes containing                                         │
│          expression()/javascript:/behavior:/-moz-binding.                    │
│        • Sensitive attributes (href, src, action, formaction, background,    │
│          xlink:href, srcdoc, data, poster) whose values begin with           │
│          javascript:, vbscript:, or certain data: URIs that embed            │
│          HTML/JS/SVG.                                                        │
│        • srcdoc attributes containing inline <script> or javascript:.        │
│     • For dangerous attributes, replaces only the exact attribute substring  │
│       with a single space (instead of deleting surrounding whitespace) to    │
│       preserve formatting as much as possible.                               │
│     • Applies all removals/replacements by byte positions on the original    │
│       text so text outside the exact removed/replaced substrings remains     │
│       identical.                                                             │
│     • Writes the modified content back in-place to the same file.            │
│                                                                              │
│ How to use                                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│  • Run: python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html                             │
│  • The input file is modified in-place. Make a backup if needed.             │
│                                                                              │
│ Design tradeoffs and limitations                                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Goal: preserve original formatting as much as possible while removing     │
│    JavaScript/XSS vectors.                                                   │
│  • This is a heuristic, regex-and-byte-span-based sanitizer. It improves     │
│    formatting preservation relative to a full parse-and-serialize approach,  │
│    but:                                                                      │
│     • It cannot guarantee detection of every obfuscated vector (e.g.,        │
│       attribute names obfuscated by character references in uncommon ways,   │
│       or extremely clever encoding mixtures).                                │
│     • It is conservative about whole-tag removals (removes entire embed      │
│       blocks), which can remove legitimate content to ensure safety.         │
│     • Complex or malformed HTML may produce missed or imprecise matches.     │
│  • For maximum semantic correctness and coverage against obfuscation, a      │
│    parser-based sanitizer (lxml/bleach) is recommended; that approach is     │
│    more robust but will usually reserialize the document and can change      │
│    whitespace, quoting, or attribute order.                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Next steps I can take (optional)                                             │

│    formatting is preserved.                                                  │
│  • Provide a parser-based (lxml) alternative if you prefer stronger          │

│ finish_reason='stop' content='Summary of what I did (step-by-step)\n-        │
│ Created and iteratively improved /app/filter.py in /app.\n- Implemented a    │
│ hybrid, byte-preserving HTML sanitizer and then improved it to a             │
│ blank-preserving removal strategy so formatting/line layout is preserved     │
│ exactly:\n  - Reads the input file as bytes and decodes with utf-8 +         │
│ surrogateescape so undecodable bytes are preserved.\n  - Identifies          │
│ dangerous whole-tag blocks by exact byte-span matches and marks them for     │
│ blanking:\n    - <script>...</script>\n    - embedding tags (iframe, object, │
│ embed, applet, frame, frameset) including self-closing forms\n    - <link    │
│ rel="import"> tags\n    - <meta http-equiv="refresh"> tags whose content     │
│ contains javascript:\n    - <style>...</style> blocks that include           │
│ expression(), url(javascript:), behavior:, -moz-binding, etc.\n  - Scans     │
│ opening tags and parses attributes (with an attribute regex) and detects     │
│ dangerous attributes by:\n    - Unescaping attribute names/values            │
│ (html.unescape) so common entity-encoded obfuscations are handled (e.g.,     │
│ o&#x6e;click -> onclick).\n    - Removing attributes whose name starts with  │
│ "on" (inline event handlers).\n    - Removing style attributes containing    │
│ expression()/javascript:/behavior:/-moz-binding.\n    - Removing sensitive   │
│ attributes (href, src, action, formaction, background, xlink:href, srcdoc,   │
│ data, poster) when their values start with javascript:, vbscript:, or        │
│ dangerous data: types (text/html, JS, SVG), and removing srcdoc when it      │
│ contains scripts.\n  - Instead of deleting those spans (which changes        │
│ byte/line layout), the script replaces each removed span with a replacement  │
│ of exactly the same length where every character is replaced by a space      │
│ except newline characters (\r and \n) which are preserved. This keeps line   │
│ breaks and column positions intact so the file formatting/visible layout     │
│ remains the same while the harmful content is blanked out.\n  - Writes the   │
│ modified content back in-place, preserving surrogate-escaped bytes.\n\nHow   │
│ to run\n- Command: python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html\n- File is       │
│ modified in-place. Make a backup if you want to retain the original.\n\nWhy  │
│ this approach\n- The user required "Do not alter the formatting of the HTML  │
│ content in any way" while removing JavaScript. Fully parsing and             │
│ reserializing HTML (lxml, bleach) is safer semantically but will usually     │
│ change whitespace/attribute quoting/ordering. The blank-preserving approach  │
│ aims to remove dangerous content while leaving visible formatting, line      │
│ breaks, and byte positions unchanged.\n\nLimitations and caveats\n-          │
│ Heuristic-based: while attribute names and values are unescaped before       │
│ checking (so many common obfuscations are detected), extremely crafty or     │
│ multilayer-encoded obfuscations could still evade detection.\n- The script   │
│ is conservative about entire-block removals (it blanks whole embed blocks),  │
│ which may remove content beyond just attribute-level fixes.\n- Malformed     │
│ HTML and extremely unusual encodings may cause misses or false negatives;    │
│ for provable correctness against all obfuscation, a parser with source-span  │
│ support (or an HTML tokenizer that yields original spans) would be           │
│ necessary.\n- This implementation prioritizes preserving visible             │
│ formatting/layout. If you prefer maximum semantic sanitization coverage over │
│ exact formatting preservation, I can provide a parser-based sanitizer        │
│ (lxml/bleach) that will more reliably remove all executable JS but may       │
│ change formatting.\n\nNext steps I can take (pick one)\n- Run the            │
│ tester&#x27;s suite of automated tests (the same test1..test8) and show      │

╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭────────────────────────────── solver - message ──────────────────────────────╮
│ Summary of what I did (step-by-step)                                         │
│                                                                              │
│  • Created and iteratively improved /app/filter.py in /app.                  │
│  • Implemented a hybrid, byte-preserving HTML sanitizer and then improved it │
│    to a blank-preserving removal strategy so formatting/line layout is       │
│    preserved exactly:                                                        │
│     • Reads the input file as bytes and decodes with utf-8 + surrogateescape │
│       so undecodable bytes are preserved.                                    │
│     • Identifies dangerous whole-tag blocks by exact byte-span matches and   │
│       marks them for blanking:                                               │
│        • <script>...</script>                                                │
│        • embedding tags (iframe, object, embed, applet, frame, frameset)     │
│          including self-closing forms                                        │
│        • <link rel="import"> tags                                            │
│        • <meta http-equiv="refresh"> tags whose content contains javascript: │
│        • <style>...</style> blocks that include expression(),                │
│          url(javascript:), behavior:, -moz-binding, etc.                     │
│     • Scans opening tags and parses attributes (with an attribute regex) and │
│       detects dangerous attributes by:                                       │
│        • Unescaping attribute names/values (html.unescape) so common         │
│          entity-encoded obfuscations are handled (e.g., o&#x6e;click ->      │
│          onclick).                                                           │
│        • Removing attributes whose name starts with "on" (inline event       │
│          handlers).                                                          │
│        • Removing style attributes containing                                │
│          expression()/javascript:/behavior:/-moz-binding.                    │
│        • Removing sensitive attributes (href, src, action, formaction,       │
│          background, xlink:href, srcdoc, data, poster) when their values     │
│          start with javascript:, vbscript:, or dangerous data: types         │
│          (text/html, JS, SVG), and removing srcdoc when it contains scripts. │
│     • Instead of deleting those spans (which changes byte/line layout), the  │
│       script replaces each removed span with a replacement of exactly the    │
│       same length where every character is replaced by a space except        │
│       newline characters (\r and \n) which are preserved. This keeps line    │
│       breaks and column positions intact so the file formatting/visible      │
│       layout remains the same while the harmful content is blanked out.      │
│     • Writes the modified content back in-place, preserving                  │
│       surrogate-escaped bytes.                                               │
│                                                                              │
│ How to run                                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│  • Command: python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html                         │
│  • File is modified in-place. Make a backup if you want to retain the        │
│    original.                                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ Why this approach                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│  • The user required "Do not alter the formatting of the HTML content in any │
│    way" while removing JavaScript. Fully parsing and reserializing HTML      │
│    (lxml, bleach) is safer semantically but will usually change              │
│    whitespace/attribute quoting/ordering. The blank-preserving approach aims │
│    to remove dangerous content while leaving visible formatting, line        │
│    breaks, and byte positions unchanged.                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ Limitations and caveats                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│  • Heuristic-based: while attribute names and values are unescaped before    │
│    checking (so many common obfuscations are detected), extremely crafty or  │
│    multilayer-encoded obfuscations could still evade detection.              │
│  • The script is conservative about entire-block removals (it blanks whole   │
│    embed blocks), which may remove content beyond just attribute-level       │
│    fixes.                                                                    │
│  • Malformed HTML and extremely unusual encodings may cause misses or false  │
│    negatives; for provable correctness against all obfuscation, a parser     │
│    with source-span support (or an HTML tokenizer that yields original       │
│    spans) would be necessary.                                                │
│  • This implementation prioritizes preserving visible formatting/layout. If  │
│    you prefer maximum semantic sanitization coverage over exact formatting   │
│    preservation, I can provide a parser-based sanitizer (lxml/bleach) that   │
│    will more reliably remove all executable JS but may change formatting.    │
│                                                                              │
│ Next steps I can take (pick one)                                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Run the tester's suite of automated tests (the same test1..test8) and     │
│    show diffs to verify that only the harmful substrings were blanked and    │
│    formatting was preserved.                                                 │
│  • Replace with a parser-based implementation (lxml) for stronger            │
│    sanitization at the cost of reserialization/formatting changes.           │
│  • Further harden detection (e.g., more aggressive unescape/normalization    │
│    tiers) if you want to keep byte-preservation but expand coverage.         │
│                                                                              │

│ <output> #!/usr/bin/env python3 """ Byte-preserving HTML sanitizer that      │
│ removes JavaScript vectors in-place while preserving file formatting (line   │
│ breaks and overall layout).                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Strategy:                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • Read the file as bytes and decode with utf-8 surrogateescape to preserve  │
│    unknown bytes.                                                            │
│  • Identify dangerous spans (script blocks, dangerous embed tags, link       │
│    rel=import, meta refresh with javascript:, style blocks with dangerous    │
│    CSS).                                                                     │
│  • Identify dangerous attributes (event handlers, style with expressions,    │
│    attributes with javascript:/vbscript:/dangerous data:, srcdoc with        │
│    scripts).                                                                 │
│  • Instead of deleting spans, replace them with a blank-preserving string of │
│    the same length where all characters except newlines (\r and \n) are      │
│    replaced with spaces. This preserves byte positions, line lengths, and    │
│    visible formatting except that harmful content is removed.                │
│                                                                              │
│ Usage: python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html """                          │
│                                                                              │
│ import sys import re import html                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ if len(sys.argv) != 2: print("Usage: filter.py path/to/file.html",           │
│ file=sys.stderr) sys.exit(2)                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ path = sys.argv[1]                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │

│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ with open(path, 'rb') as f: raw = f.read() try: orig = raw.decode('utf-8',   │
│ 'surrogateescape') except Exception: orig = raw.decode('latin-1')            │
│                                                                              │
│ length = len(orig) removals = []  # list of (start, end, replacement_flag)   │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃         If replacement_flag is a string -> use it as replacement         ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ def blank_preserve(s): # preserve newlines, replace other chars with spaces  │
│ to keep layout return ''.join(ch if ch in '\r\n' else ' ' for ch in s)       │
│                                                                              │
│ def mark_removal(start, end, replacement_flag=None): if start < 0: start = 0 │
│ if end > len(orig): end = len(orig) if start >= end: return                  │
│ removals.append((start, end, replacement_flag))                              │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     Helper to unescape and normalize attribute value/name for checks     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ def norm_unescape(s): if s is None: return '' try: return                    │
│ html.unescape(s).strip() except Exception: return s.strip()                  │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                  1) Find and mark <script>...</script>                   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ for m in re.finditer(r'(?is)<script\b[^>]>.?</script\s*>', orig):            │
│ mark_removal(m.start(), m.end(), None)                                       │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                       2) Dangerous embedding tags                        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ embed_tags = ['iframe','object','embed','applet','frame','frameset'] for tag │
│ in embed_tags: patt = re.compile(r'(?is)<%s\b[^>]>.?</%s\s*>' %              │
│ (re.escape(tag), re.escape(tag))) for m in patt.finditer(orig):              │
│ mark_removal(m.start(), m.end(), None) patt2 =                               │
│ re.compile(r'(?is)<%s\b[^>]/\s>' % re.escape(tag)) for m in                  │
│ patt2.finditer(orig): mark_removal(m.start(), m.end(), None)                 │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │

│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ norm_unescape(val).lower() == 'import': mark_removal(m.start(), m.end(),     │
│ None)                                                                        │

│ ┃             5) style blocks containing dangerous constructs              ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ and 'javascript:' in low): mark_removal(m.start(), m.end(), None)            │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                    Helper to detect dangerous schemes                    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ def value_has_dangerous_scheme(val): if val is None: return False v =        │
│ norm_unescape(val).lower() if v.startswith(('javascript:', 'vbscript:')):    │
│ return True if v.startswith('data:'): if any(t in v for t in                 │
│ ('text/html','text/javascript','application/javascript','application/ecmascr │
│ ipt','image/svg+xml','image/svg+')): return True if 'script' in v or         │
│ '<script' in v: return True return False                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                       Build removed intervals list                       ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ removed_intervals = [] for s,e,_ in removals:                                │
│ removed_intervals.append((s,e))                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ def overlaps_removed(start, end): for a,b in removed_intervals: if start < b │
│ and end > a: return True return False                                        │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    6) Scan tags and attributes; mark attribute spans for blanking if     ┃ │
│ ┃                                dangerous                                 ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ for m in re.finditer(r'(?is)<([a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9:_-])\b([^>])>', orig):      │
│ tag_start = m.start() tag_end = m.end() if overlaps_removed(tag_start,       │
│ tag_end): continue attrs_text = m.group(2) attrs_rel_base = m.start(2)       │
│ attr_re =                                                                    │
│ re.compile(r'(\s+)([^\s=<>/]+)(\s*(=)\s*("([^"])"|&#x27;([^&#x27;])&#x27;|([ │
│ ^\s>]+)))?', re.DOTALL) for am in attr_re.finditer(attrs_text): raw_name =   │
│ am.group(2) val = None if am.group(6) is not None: val = am.group(6) elif    │
│ am.group(7) is not None: val = am.group(7) elif am.group(8) is not None: val │
│ = am.group(8) attr_abs_start = attrs_rel_base + am.start() attr_abs_end =    │
│ attrs_rel_base + am.end() # Unescape name un_name =                          │
│ norm_unescape(raw_name).lower() remove_attr = False if                       │
│ un_name.startswith('on'): remove_attr = True else: if un_name == 'style': if │
│ val is not None: vlow = norm_unescape(val).lower() if 'expression(' in vlow  │
│ or 'javascript:' in vlow or 'behavior:' in vlow or '-moz-binding' in vlow:   │
│ remove_attr = True sensitive =                                               │
│ {'href','src','action','formaction','background','xlink:href','srcdoc','data │
│ ','poster'} if un_name in sensitive: if value_has_dangerous_scheme(val):     │
│ remove_attr = True elif un_name == 'srcdoc' and val is not None: if          │
│ '<script' in val.lower() or 'javascript:' in norm_unescape(val).lower():     │
│ remove_attr = True if remove_attr: if overlaps_removed(attr_abs_start,       │
│ attr_abs_end): continue mark_removal(attr_abs_start, attr_abs_end, None)     │
│ removed_intervals.append((attr_abs_start, attr_abs_end))                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃ 7) Neutralize remaining javascript: schemes occurrences in attributes by ┃ │
│ ┃                       blanking the scheme portion                        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ for m in re.finditer(r'(?i)=\s*("|&#x27;)?\s*(javascript\s*:\s*)', orig): s  │
│ = m.start(2) e = m.end(2) if overlaps_removed(s, e): continue                │
│ mark_removal(s, e, None)                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                      Consolidate and merge removals                      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ removals.sort(key=lambda x: x[0]) merged = [] for s,e,r in removals: if not  │
│ merged: merged.append([s,e,r]) else: last = merged[-1] if s <= last[1]: #    │
│ merge last[1] = max(last[1], e) # if either requests a concrete replacement  │
│ string, keep the earliest non-None if last[2] is None and r is not None:     │
│ last[2] = r else: merged.append([s,e,r])                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃         Build output by applying replacements from end to start          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ out_parts = [] idx = len(orig) for s,e,r in reversed(merged): if e > idx: e  │
│ = idx # append tail out_parts.append(orig[e:idx]) # prepare replacement if r │
│ is None: repl = blank_preserve(orig[s:e]) else: repl = r                     │
│ out_parts.append(repl) idx = s out_parts.append(orig[0:idx]) out_text =      │
│ ''.join(reversed(out_parts))                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                  Write back preserving surrogateescape                   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ with open(path, 'wb') as f: f.write(out_text.encode('utf-8',                 │
│ 'surrogateescape'))                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ if name == 'main': pass </output>                                            │

│ print(&#x27;  orig context:&#x27;, orig[start:end])\n        print(&#x27;    │
│ out  context:&#x27;, out[start:end])\n    else:\n        print(&#x27;  no    │
│ byte diffs&#x27;)\n    # textual checks (case-insensitive)\n    low_out =    │
│ out.decode(&#x27;utf-8&#x27;,&#x27;surrogateescape&#x27;).lower()\n    for   │
│ ind in indicators:\n        print(f"  contains &#x27;{ind}&#x27;?", ind in   │
│ low_out)\n    # also check percent-encoded javascript in decoded form: leave │
│ as-is\n    print(&#x27;\n&#x27;)\nPY\n', 'enter': True}                      │

│ values, but avoid infinite loops.\n  - Only apply percent-decoding for       │

│ verified\n\nWhat I created/updated\n- /app/filter.py — an in-place HTML      │
│ sanitizer that removes JavaScript/XSS vectors while preserving visible       │
│ formatting and file byte layout.\n- Key implementation details:\n  - Reads   │
│ the file as bytes and decodes with UTF-8 + surrogateescape so undecodable    │
│ bytes are preserved.\n  - Detects dangerous whole-tag blocks by byte-span    │
│ and marks them for removal: <script>, embedding tags                         │
│ (iframe/object/embed/applet/frame/frameset), <link rel="import">, <meta      │
│ http-equiv="refresh"> with javascript:, and <style> blocks that include      │
│ expression()/url(javascript:)/behavior/-moz-binding.\n  - Scans opening tags │
│ and attributes; unescapes attribute names and values to detect obfuscated    │
│ names (e.g., o&#x6e;click) and dangerous values.\n  - Normalization includes │
│ html.unescape plus percent-decoding (urllib.parse.unquote) applied           │
│ iteratively (up to 3 times) to detect percent-encoded schemes such as        │
│ jav%61script: or fully percent-encoded javascript:.\n  - Removal strategy:   │
│ blank-preserving replacement — every removed character is replaced with a    │
│ space except newline chars (\r and \n) which are preserved. This keeps line  │
│ counts, line breaks, and column positions identical while harmful text is    │
│ erased.\n  - Writes output back in-place using surrogateescape so other      │
│ bytes are preserved.\n\nWhat I tested\n- Ran targeted obfuscation/edge-case  │
│ tests:\n  - Character-reference obfuscated attribute names: o&#x6e;click and │
│ o&#110;click — removed.\n  - SVG event attributes (onload): removed.\n  -    │
│ srcdoc with escaped <script>: removed.\n  - Percent-encoded schemes:         │
│ jav%61script and fully percent-encoded %6a%61... were detected and removed   │
│ after adding percent-decoding.\n- Verification: file lengths before/after    │
│ were preserved in tests (blank-preserving approach), and tests that          │
│ previously evaded detection (percent-encoded) are now                        │
│ handled.\n\nLimitations and caveats\n- Heuristic approach: robust against    │
│ many practical obfuscations (char refs, percent-encoding, multiple decoding  │
│ passes), but cannot guarantee coverage for every possible layered/novel      │
│ obfuscation or malformed HTML. No regex-based sanitizer can be provably      │
│ perfect.\n- Conservative removals: some whole-tag blanking may remove        │
│ legitimate embedded content (blanked in-place). This was a design choice to  │
│ avoid introducing a new JS vector.\n- Performance: works for typical files;  │
│ extremely large files or streaming requirements were not stress-tested       │
│ here.\n- No logging/dry-run by default: the script currently modifies files  │
│ in-place without producing a removal audit log.\n\nUsage\n- Run in-place:    │
│ python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html\n- Recommended: back up files first │
│ if you need the original contents.\n\nOptional next actions I can take\n-    │
│ Add a dry-run / verbose mode that prints/logs the exact byte spans that      │
│ would be blanked (auditability).\n- Run a larger fuzz/test corpus (mixed     │
│ encodings, MathML/SVG, namespaces) and produce binary diffs to further       │
│ validate exact-byte behavior across many samples.\n- Provide a parser-based  │
│ alternative (lxml/bleach) if you prefer semantic robustness over exact       │
│ formatting preservation.\n\nIf you want me to run further tests (fuzzing,    │

│ Summary of what I implemented and verified                                   │
│                                                                              │
│ What I created/updated                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│  • /app/filter.py — an in-place HTML sanitizer that removes JavaScript/XSS   │
│    vectors while preserving visible formatting and file byte layout.         │
│  • Key implementation details:                                               │
│     • Reads the file as bytes and decodes with UTF-8 + surrogateescape so    │
│       undecodable bytes are preserved.                                       │
│     • Detects dangerous whole-tag blocks by byte-span and marks them for     │
│       removal: <script>, embedding tags                                      │
│       (iframe/object/embed/applet/frame/frameset), <link rel="import">,      │
│       <meta http-equiv="refresh"> with javascript:, and <style> blocks that  │
│       include expression()/url(javascript:)/behavior/-moz-binding.           │
│     • Scans opening tags and attributes; unescapes attribute names and       │
│       values to detect obfuscated names (e.g., o&#x6e;click) and dangerous   │
│       values.                                                                │
│     • Normalization includes html.unescape plus percent-decoding             │
│       (urllib.parse.unquote) applied iteratively (up to 3 times) to detect   │
│       percent-encoded schemes such as jav%61script: or fully percent-encoded │
│       javascript:.                                                           │
│     • Removal strategy: blank-preserving replacement — every removed         │
│       character is replaced with a space except newline chars (\r and \n)    │
│       which are preserved. This keeps line counts, line breaks, and column   │
│       positions identical while harmful text is erased.                      │
│     • Writes output back in-place using surrogateescape so other bytes are   │
│       preserved.                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ What I tested                                                                │
│                                                                              │
│  • Ran targeted obfuscation/edge-case tests:                                 │
│     • Character-reference obfuscated attribute names: o&#x6e;click and       │
│       o&#110;click — removed.                                                │
│     • SVG event attributes (onload): removed.                                │
│     • srcdoc with escaped <script>: removed.                                 │
│     • Percent-encoded schemes: jav%61script and fully percent-encoded        │
│       %6a%61... were detected and removed after adding percent-decoding.     │
│  • Verification: file lengths before/after were preserved in tests           │
│    (blank-preserving approach), and tests that previously evaded detection   │
│    (percent-encoded) are now handled.                                        │
│                                                                              │
│ Limitations and caveats                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│  • Heuristic approach: robust against many practical obfuscations (char      │
│    refs, percent-encoding, multiple decoding passes), but cannot guarantee   │
│    coverage for every possible layered/novel obfuscation or malformed HTML.  │
│    No regex-based sanitizer can be provably perfect.                         │
│  • Conservative removals: some whole-tag blanking may remove legitimate      │
│    embedded content (blanked in-place). This was a design choice to avoid    │
│    introducing a new JS vector.                                              │
│  • Performance: works for typical files; extremely large files or streaming  │
│    requirements were not stress-tested here.                                 │
│  • No logging/dry-run by default: the script currently modifies files        │
│    in-place without producing a removal audit log.                           │
│                                                                              │
│ Usage                                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  • Run in-place: python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html                    │
│  • Recommended: back up files first if you need the original contents.       │
│                                                                              │
│ Optional next actions I can take                                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Add a dry-run / verbose mode that prints/logs the exact byte spans that   │
│    would be blanked (auditability).                                          │
│  • Run a larger fuzz/test corpus (mixed encodings, MathML/SVG, namespaces)   │
│    and produce binary diffs to further validate exact-byte behavior across   │
│    many samples.                                                             │
│  • Provide a parser-based alternative (lxml/bleach) if you prefer semantic   │
│    robustness over exact formatting preservation.                            │
│                                                                              │
│ If you want me to run further tests (fuzzing, binary diffs, or enable a      │
│ dry-run mode), tell me which and I'll proceed.                               │
