│ Tool: Shell                                                                  │
│ Is error: False                                                              │
│ Content:                                                                     │
│ <exit-code> 0 </exit-code>                                                   │
│ <output> #!/usr/bin/env python3 """ Byte-preserving HTML sanitizer that      │
│ removes JavaScript vectors in-place while preserving file formatting (line   │
│ breaks and overall layout).                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Strategy:                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • Read the file as bytes and decode with utf-8 surrogateescape to preserve  │
│    unknown bytes.                                                            │
│  • Identify dangerous spans (script blocks, dangerous embed tags, link       │
│    rel=import, meta refresh with javascript:, style blocks with dangerous    │
│    CSS).                                                                     │
│  • Identify dangerous attributes (event handlers, style with expressions,    │
│    attributes with javascript:/vbscript:/dangerous data:, srcdoc with        │
│    scripts).                                                                 │
│  • Instead of deleting spans, replace them with a blank-preserving string of │
│    the same length where all characters except newlines (\r and \n) are      │
│    replaced with spaces. This preserves byte positions, line lengths, and    │
│    visible formatting except that harmful content is removed.                │
│                                                                              │
│ Usage: python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html """                          │
│                                                                              │
│ import sys import re import html                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ if len(sys.argv) != 2: print("Usage: filter.py path/to/file.html",           │
│ file=sys.stderr) sys.exit(2)                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ path = sys.argv[1]                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃          Read raw bytes and decode preserving undecodable bytes          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ with open(path, 'rb') as f: raw = f.read() try: orig = raw.decode('utf-8',   │
│ 'surrogateescape') except Exception: orig = raw.decode('latin-1')            │
│                                                                              │
│ length = len(orig) removals = []  # list of (start, end, replacement_flag)   │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃  If replacement_flag is None -> create blank-preserving replacement of   ┃ │
│ ┃                               same length                                ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃         If replacement_flag is a string -> use it as replacement         ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ def blank_preserve(s): # preserve newlines, replace other chars with spaces  │
│ to keep layout return ''.join(ch if ch in '\r\n' else ' ' for ch in s)       │
│                                                                              │
│ def mark_removal(start, end, replacement_flag=None): if start < 0: start = 0 │
│ if end > len(orig): end = len(orig) if start >= end: return                  │
│ removals.append((start, end, replacement_flag))                              │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     Helper to unescape and normalize attribute value/name for checks     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ import urllib.parse                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ def norm_unescape(s): if s is None: return '' try: # First HTML-unescape     │
│ (handles &amp; & #x.. etc.) v = html.unescape(s) except Exception: v = s v = │
│ v.strip() # Percent-decode iteratively up to 3 times to catch common         │
│ double-encoding tricks try: prev = None i = 0 while i < 3 and v != prev:     │
│ prev = v v = urllib.parse.unquote(v) i += 1 except Exception: pass return v  │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                  1) Find and mark <script>...</script>                   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ for m in re.finditer(r'(?is)<script\b[^>]>.?</script\s*>', orig):            │
│ mark_removal(m.start(), m.end(), None)                                       │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                       2) Dangerous embedding tags                        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ embed_tags = ['iframe','object','embed','applet','frame','frameset'] for tag │
│ in embed_tags: patt = re.compile(r'(?is)<%s\b[^>]>.?</%s\s*>' %              │
│ (re.escape(tag), re.escape(tag))) for m in patt.finditer(orig):              │
│ mark_removal(m.start(), m.end(), None) patt2 =                               │
│ re.compile(r'(?is)<%s\b[^>]/\s>' % re.escape(tag)) for m in                  │
│ patt2.finditer(orig): mark_removal(m.start(), m.end(), None)                 │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                           3) <link rel=import>                           ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ for m in re.finditer(r'(?is)<link\b[^>]>', orig): tag_text = m.group(0) relm │
│ = re.search(r'(?is)\brel\s=\s*("([^"])"|&#x27;([^&#x27;])&#x27;|([^\s>]+))', │
│ tag_text) if relm: val = relm.group(2) if relm.group(2) is not None else     │
│ (relm.group(3) if relm.group(3) is not None else relm.group(4)) if val and   │
│ norm_unescape(val).lower() == 'import': mark_removal(m.start(), m.end(),     │
│ None)                                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                     4) meta refresh with javascript:                     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ for m in re.finditer(r'(?is)<meta\b[^>]>', orig): tag_text = m.group(0) if   │
│ re.search(r'(?is)http-equiv\s=\s*("|&#x27;)?refresh\1', tag_text): cont =    │
│ re.search(r'(?is)\bcontent\s*=\s*("([^"])"|&#x27;([^&#x27;])&#x27;|([^\s>]+) │
│ )', tag_text) if cont: val = cont.group(2) if cont.group(2) is not None else │
│ (cont.group(3) if cont.group(3) is not None else cont.group(4)) if val and   │
│ 'javascript:' in norm_unescape(val).lower(): mark_removal(m.start(),         │
│ m.end(), None)                                                               │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃             5) style blocks containing dangerous constructs              ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ for m in re.finditer(r'(?is)<style\b[^>]>(.?)</style\s*>', orig): content =  │
│ m.group(1) low = content.lower() if ('expression(' in low) or ('javascript:' │
│ in low) or ('behavior:' in low) or ('-moz-binding' in low) or ('url(' in low │
│ and 'javascript:' in low): mark_removal(m.start(), m.end(), None)            │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                    Helper to detect dangerous schemes                    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ def value_has_dangerous_scheme(val): if val is None: return False v =        │
│ norm_unescape(val).lower() if v.startswith(('javascript:', 'vbscript:')):    │
│ return True if v.startswith('data:'): if any(t in v for t in                 │
│ ('text/html','text/javascript','application/javascript','application/ecmascr │
│ ipt','image/svg+xml','image/svg+')): return True if 'script' in v or         │
│ '<script' in v: return True return False                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                       Build removed intervals list                       ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ removed_intervals = [] for s,e,_ in removals:                                │
│ removed_intervals.append((s,e))                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ def overlaps_removed(start, end): for a,b in removed_intervals: if start < b │
│ and end > a: return True return False                                        │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    6) Scan tags and attributes; mark attribute spans for blanking if     ┃ │
│ ┃                                dangerous                                 ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ for m in re.finditer(r'(?is)<([a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9:_-])\b([^>])>', orig):      │
│ tag_start = m.start() tag_end = m.end() if overlaps_removed(tag_start,       │
│ tag_end): continue attrs_text = m.group(2) attrs_rel_base = m.start(2)       │
│ attr_re =                                                                    │
│ re.compile(r'(\s+)([^\s=<>/]+)(\s*(=)\s*("([^"])"|&#x27;([^&#x27;])&#x27;|([ │
│ ^\s>]+)))?', re.DOTALL) for am in attr_re.finditer(attrs_text): raw_name =   │
│ am.group(2) val = None if am.group(6) is not None: val = am.group(6) elif    │
│ am.group(7) is not None: val = am.group(7) elif am.group(8) is not None: val │
│ = am.group(8) attr_abs_start = attrs_rel_base + am.start() attr_abs_end =    │
│ attrs_rel_base + am.end() # Unescape name un_name =                          │
│ norm_unescape(raw_name).lower() remove_attr = False if                       │
│ un_name.startswith('on'): remove_attr = True else: if un_name == 'style': if │
│ val is not None: vlow = norm_unescape(val).lower() if 'expression(' in vlow  │
│ or 'javascript:' in vlow or 'behavior:' in vlow or '-moz-binding' in vlow:   │
│ remove_attr = True sensitive =                                               │
│ {'href','src','action','formaction','background','xlink:href','srcdoc','data │
│ ','poster'} if un_name in sensitive: if value_has_dangerous_scheme(val):     │
│ remove_attr = True elif un_name == 'srcdoc' and val is not None: if          │
│ '<script' in val.lower() or 'javascript:' in norm_unescape(val).lower():     │
│ remove_attr = True if remove_attr: if overlaps_removed(attr_abs_start,       │
│ attr_abs_end): continue mark_removal(attr_abs_start, attr_abs_end, None)     │
│ removed_intervals.append((attr_abs_start, attr_abs_end))                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃ 7) Neutralize remaining javascript: schemes occurrences in attributes by ┃ │
│ ┃                       blanking the scheme portion                        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ for m in re.finditer(r'(?i)=\s*("|&#x27;)?\s*(javascript\s*:\s*)', orig): s  │
│ = m.start(2) e = m.end(2) if overlaps_removed(s, e): continue                │
│ mark_removal(s, e, None)                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                      Consolidate and merge removals                      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ removals.sort(key=lambda x: x[0]) merged = [] for s,e,r in removals: if not  │
│ merged: merged.append([s,e,r]) else: last = merged[-1] if s <= last[1]: #    │
│ merge last[1] = max(last[1], e) # if either requests a concrete replacement  │
│ string, keep the earliest non-None if last[2] is None and r is not None:     │
│ last[2] = r else: merged.append([s,e,r])                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃         Build output by applying replacements from end to start          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ out_parts = [] idx = len(orig) for s,e,r in reversed(merged): if e > idx: e  │
│ = idx # append tail out_parts.append(orig[e:idx]) # prepare replacement if r │
│ is None: repl = blank_preserve(orig[s:e]) else: repl = r                     │
│ out_parts.append(repl) idx = s out_parts.append(orig[0:idx]) out_text =      │
│ ''.join(reversed(out_parts))                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                  Write back preserving surrogateescape                   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ with open(path, 'wb') as f: f.write(out_text.encode('utf-8',                 │
│ 'surrogateescape'))                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ if name == 'main': pass </output>                                            │

│ with an iterative, bidirectional decoding loop:\n  - Apply percent-decoding  │

│ norm_unescape(s):\n    - v = s\n    - repeat up to N times:\n      - v_prev  │
│ = v\n      - v = urllib.parse.unquote(v)\n      - v = html.unescape(v)\n     │

│ Why the task is not fully solved ([solver])                                  │
│                                                                              │
│  • The user's strict requirement is that formatting must not be altered and  │
│    that the script must remove JavaScript in all realistic forms. While the  │
│    tool preserves length/newlines and handles many obfuscations, it does not │
│    yet detect every layered encoding. Concretely:                            │
│     • It fails to detect attribute names where the entity markers themselves │
│       were percent-encoded (e.g., o%26%23x6e%3bclick). This is a realistic   │
│       obfuscation path that can bypass the current checks.                   │
│     • There remain plausible edge encodings (other multilayered mixes of     │
│       percent-encoding and char refs) that are not yet exhaustively          │
│       decoded/normalized.                                                    │
│  • Because at least one practical obfuscation still bypasses the filter, the │
│    overall goal ("remove all JavaScript while preserving formatting") is not │
│    fully achieved.                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│ Recommended concise fix                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│  • Normalize attribute names and values with an iterative, bidirectional     │
│    decoding loop:                                                            │
│     • Apply percent-decoding and HTML-unescape repeatedly until the string   │
│       stabilizes or a safe iteration cap (e.g., 3–5 passes) is reached.      │
│     • Example algorithm for norm_unescape(s):                                │
│        • v = s                                                               │
│        • repeat up to N times:                                               │
│           • v_prev = v                                                       │
│           • v = urllib.parse.unquote(v)                                      │
│           • v = html.unescape(v)                                             │
│           • if v == v_prev: break                                            │
│        • return v.strip()                                                    │
│     • Use this for both attribute names and values before detection checks.  │
│  • Keep the blank-preserving write behavior (so formatting/line layout       │
│    remains unchanged).                                                       │
│  • Re-run the obfuscation/fuzz tests (including pe5) and verify byte-length  │
│    equality and that the earlier escaping bypass is closed.                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Next steps I can take for you                                                │
│                                                                              │
│  • Implement the iterative percent-decode + html.unescape ordering in        │
│    /app/filter.py (attribute names and values), re-run the obf/pe test       │
│    suite, and report results (byte-length checks and diffs).                 │
│  • Optionally add a dry-run/verbose mode to log exact byte spans replaced    │
│    for auditability.                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│ Status                                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│  • Not fully solved; one concrete obfuscation class bypasses the sanitizer.  │
│    See recommendations above to close this gap.                              │
│                                                                              │
│ Tag: [solver]                                                                │

│ (Solver)\n\nWhat I implemented\n- Created and iteratively improved           │
│ /app/filter.py — an in-place HTML sanitizer that removes JavaScript/XSS      │
│ vectors while preserving visible formatting and original byte layout as much │
│ as practical.\n- Key design choices:\n  - Byte-preserving, blank-preserving  │
│ removals: any removed span is replaced by a string of the exact same length  │
│ where non-newline characters are replaced by spaces and newline characters   │
│ are preserved. This keeps file length, line breaks, and column positions     │
│ identical while removing harmful content.\n  - Detection heuristics (applied │
│ to exact byte spans in the original file):\n    - Whole-block removal        │
│ (blanking) for <script>...</script>, dangerous <style> blocks (expression(), │
│ url(javascript:), behavior:, -moz-binding), embedding tags (<iframe>,        │
│ <object>, <embed>, <applet>, <frame>, <frameset>), <link rel="import">, and  │
│ <meta http-equiv="refresh"> when it contains javascript:.\n    -             │

│ "on"), style attributes with dangerous CSS, and sensitive attributes (href,  │
│ src, action, formaction, background, xlink:href, srcdoc, data, poster) whose │
│ values indicate dangerous schemes (javascript:, vbscript:, or data: types    │
│ likely to embed HTML/JS/SVG).\n  - Robust normalization for detection:\n     │
│ - norm_unescape now applies iterative percent-decoding                       │
│ (urllib.parse.unquote) followed by html.unescape up to a fixed iteration cap │
│ (5), so it detects layered obfuscations such as:\n      - HTML char refs in  │
│ attribute names (o&#x6e;click)\n      - Percent-encoded characters in        │
│ schemes (jav%61script:)\n      - Percent-encoded entity sequences            │
│ (o%26%23x6e%3bclick -> o&#x6e;click -> onclick)\n    - Normalization is used │
│ only for detection; original bytes are untouched except where blanking is    │
│ applied.\n\nWhat I tested\n- Ran a focused suite of test cases (created      │
│ temporary files and ran python3 /app/filter.py on them), including:\n  -     │
│ Basic vectors: <script>, onclick/on* attributes, javascript: hrefs           │
│ (quoted/unquoted), style blocks with url(javascript:)/behavior,              │
│ iframe/object/embed, meta refresh with javascript:, srcdoc with inline       │
│ scripts, data: URIs embedding HTML/JS/SVG.\n  - Obfuscation cases: HTML char │
│ refs in attribute names, decimal/hex char refs, percent-encoded letters in   │
│ scheme (jav%61script:), fully percent-encoded scheme (%6a%61...),            │
│ percent-encoded entity markers (o%26%23x6e%3bclick), SVG event attributes,   │
│ and double-encoded sequences.\n- Verified:\n  - Dangerous constructs were    │
│ blanked in-place.\n  - File lengths and newline positions were preserved in  │
│ tests.\n  - Initial gap (percent-encoded schemes) was fixed by adding        │
│ percent-decoding.\n  - Layered obfuscation case (percent-encoded entity) was │
│ fixed by iterating percent-decode + html.unescape.\n\nCurrent status and     │
│ limitations\n- The filter now removes many practical XSS vectors and handles │
│ several classes of obfuscation while preserving formatting (line breaks and  │
│ column positions).\n- Limitations to be aware of:\n  - This remains a        │
│ heuristic approach; no regex/tokenization approach can be mathematically     │
│ proven to catch every possible obfuscation. Extremely novel or multi-stage   │
│ obfuscation could still evade detection.\n  - The script blanks entire embed │
│ blocks conservatively; this can remove legitimate content (design            │
│ tradeoff).\n  - Performance/streaming for very large files has not been      │
│ stress-tested.\n  - No automatic audit log by default. I can add a           │

│ <output> #!/usr/bin/env python3 """ Byte-preserving HTML sanitizer that      │
│ removes JavaScript vectors in-place while preserving file formatting (line   │
│ breaks and overall layout).                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Strategy:                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • Read the file as bytes and decode with utf-8 surrogateescape to preserve  │
│    unknown bytes.                                                            │
│  • Identify dangerous spans (script blocks, dangerous embed tags, link       │
│    rel=import, meta refresh with javascript:, style blocks with dangerous    │
│    CSS).                                                                     │
│  • Identify dangerous attributes (event handlers, style with expressions,    │
│    attributes with javascript:/vbscript:/dangerous data:, srcdoc with        │
│    scripts).                                                                 │
│  • Instead of deleting spans, replace them with a blank-preserving string of │
│    the same length where all characters except newlines (\r and \n) are      │
│    replaced with spaces. This preserves byte positions, line lengths, and    │
│    visible formatting except that harmful content is removed.                │
│                                                                              │
│ Usage: python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html """                          │
│                                                                              │
│ import sys import re import html                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ if len(sys.argv) != 2: print("Usage: filter.py path/to/file.html",           │
│ file=sys.stderr) sys.exit(2)                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ path = sys.argv[1]                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃          Read raw bytes and decode preserving undecodable bytes          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ with open(path, 'rb') as f: raw = f.read() try: orig = raw.decode('utf-8',   │
│ 'surrogateescape') except Exception: orig = raw.decode('latin-1')            │
│                                                                              │
│ length = len(orig) removals = []  # list of (start, end, replacement_flag)   │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃  If replacement_flag is None -> create blank-preserving replacement of   ┃ │
│ ┃                               same length                                ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃         If replacement_flag is a string -> use it as replacement         ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ def blank_preserve(s): # preserve newlines, replace other chars with spaces  │
│ to keep layout return ''.join(ch if ch in '\r\n' else ' ' for ch in s)       │
│                                                                              │
│ def mark_removal(start, end, replacement_flag=None): if start < 0: start = 0 │
│ if end > len(orig): end = len(orig) if start >= end: return                  │
│ removals.append((start, end, replacement_flag))                              │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     Helper to unescape and normalize attribute value/name for checks     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ import urllib.parse                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ def norm_unescape(s): if s is None: return '' try: v = s except Exception: v │
│ = s if s is not None else '' v = str(v) # Iteratively apply percent-decoding │
│ then HTML unescape up to 5 times to catch layered encodings try: prev = None │
│ i = 0 while i < 5 and v != prev: prev = v # percent-decode try: v =          │
│ urllib.parse.unquote(v) except Exception: pass # html unescape try: v =      │
│ html.unescape(v) except Exception: pass i += 1 except Exception: pass return │
│ v.strip()                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │

│                                                                              │
│ removals.sort(key=lambda x: x[0]) merged = [] for s,e,r in removals: if not  │
│ merged: merged.append([s,e,r]) else: last = merged[-1] if s <= last[1]: #    │
│ merge last[1] = max(last[1], e) # if either requests a concrete replacement  │
│ string, keep the earliest non-None if last[2] is None and r is not None:     │
│ last[2] = r else: merged.append([s,e,r])                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃         Build output by applying replacements from end to start          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ out_parts = [] idx = len(orig) for s,e,r in reversed(merged): if e > idx: e  │
│ = idx # append tail out_parts.append(orig[e:idx]) # prepare replacement if r │
│ is None: repl = blank_preserve(orig[s:e]) else: repl = r                     │
│ out_parts.append(repl) idx = s out_parts.append(orig[0:idx]) out_text =      │
│ ''.join(reversed(out_parts))                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │

│                                                                              │
│ with open(path, 'wb') as f: f.write(out_text.encode('utf-8',                 │

│ finish_reason='stop' content="What I tested (steps)\n- Inspected             │
│ /app/filter.py to confirm it implements a byte-preserving, blanking          │
│ sanitizer.\n- Ran multiple targeted test suites exercising common and        │
│ obfuscated XSS vectors:\n  1. Basic vectors: <script> blocks, inline on*     │
│ attributes, javascript: hrefs (quoted/unquoted), style blocks with           │
│ url(javascript:)/expression()/behavior:, iframe/object/embed, meta refresh   │
│ with javascript:, srcdoc containing scripts, and dangerous data: URIs.\n  2. │
│ HTML entity obfuscation: attribute-names encoded with char refs              │
│ (o&#x6e;click, o&#110;click).\n  3. Percent-encoding obfuscation:            │
│ jav%61script:, fully percent-encoded javascript (%6a%61...), mixed           │
│ encodings, double-encoded sequences.\n  4. Layered encodings:                │
│ percent-encoded entity markers (o%26%23x6e%3bclick), mixed char-ref +        │
│ percent-encoding, and SVG event attributes.\n- For each test file I ran      │
│ python3 /app/filter.py file and then compared original vs output (byte       │
│ lengths, first byte diffs, and textual checks).\n\nWhat I observed           │
│ (evidence)\n- The script blanks (replaces with spaces but preserves newline  │
│ characters) all detected dangerous spans, so:\n  - All tested <script>       │
│ blocks, on* attributes, javascript:/vbscript: schemes, dangerous style       │
│ constructs, iframes/embeds/objects, meta refresh with javascript:, srcdoc    │
│ inline scripts, and many data: cases were removed (blanked).\n  - The        │
│ sanitizer preserved file length and newline positions in all tests           │
│ (out_length == orig_length), i.e., visible formatting and line layout are    │
│ preserved.\n- Obfuscation handling:\n  - HTML char ref obfuscation in        │
│ attribute names was detected and blanked.\n  - Percent-encoded javascript    │
│ schemes (single & fully encoded) and mixed/double encodings were detected    │
│ after adding iterative percent-decoding.\n  - Layered obfuscations where     │
│ percent-encoding encodes an entity reference were handled by iterating       │
│ percent-decode + html.unescape until stable (cap applied).\n- Remaining      │
│ verification: hexdump was attempted earlier but unavailable; however,        │
│ byte-length equality and first-diff inspections demonstrate preservation of  │
│ length and newline positions.\n\nAssessment and conclusion\n- The script     │
│ meets the user's functional goals in the tested cases:\n  - It takes a       │
│ filename as argv[1], modifies the file in-place, and removes many JavaScript │
│ vectors.\n  - It preserves visible formatting and byte-layout by blanking    │
│ removed substrings rather than reserializing or deleting lines.\n- Caveats   │
│ (documented, not blockers for approval):\n  - This is a heuristic approach.  │
│ Although it now handles many layered obfuscation patterns (char refs,        │
│ percent-encoding, double-encoding, mixed encodings), no                      │
│ regex/tokenization-based sanitizer can be proven to catch every possible     │
│ obfuscation. Extremely novel or deeply multi-stage encodings could still     │
│ evade detection.\n  - The implementation is conservative in some removals    │
│ (blanking whole embed blocks), which can remove legitimate content; this was │
│ a deliberate safety/design tradeoff.\n  - Performance/streaming on very      │
│ large files wasn't stress-tested here; it's intended for typical HTML        │
│ files.\n  - If absolute semantic correctness (covering every edge-case) is   │
│ required, using a parser/tokenizer that exposes source spans or a            │
│ purpose-built sanitizer library with formal guarantees is recommended (at    │
│ the cost of reserialization).\n\nFinal verdict\n- The implementation         │
│ functions as required for practical, real-world cases and preserves          │
│ formatting as specified in a byte-preserving manner. Given the tests and     │
│ fixes applied (including iterative percent-decode + entity-unescape), I      │
│ judge the task completed successfully.\n\nTag: [approver]\n\nNotes for next  │
│ actions (optional)\n- If you want, I can:\n  - Add a dry-run / verbose mode  │
│ that reports exact byte ranges blanked for audit.\n  - Run a larger          │
│ fuzz/corpus of real-world HTML to further stress-test edge cases.\n  -       │
│ Provide a parser-based alternative (lxml/bleach) if you prefer stronger      │
│ semantic removal over strict formatting preservation."                       │

│  • Inspected /app/filter.py to confirm it implements a byte-preserving,      │
│    blanking sanitizer.                                                       │
│  • Ran multiple targeted test suites exercising common and obfuscated XSS    │
│    vectors:                                                                  │
│     1 Basic vectors: <script> blocks, inline on* attributes, javascript:     │
│       hrefs (quoted/unquoted), style blocks with                             │
│       url(javascript:)/expression()/behavior:, iframe/object/embed, meta     │
│       refresh with javascript:, srcdoc containing scripts, and dangerous     │
│       data: URIs.                                                            │
│     2 HTML entity obfuscation: attribute-names encoded with char refs        │
│       (o&#x6e;click, o&#110;click).                                          │
│     3 Percent-encoding obfuscation: jav%61script:, fully percent-encoded     │
│       javascript (%6a%61...), mixed encodings, double-encoded sequences.     │
│     4 Layered encodings: percent-encoded entity markers                      │
│       (o%26%23x6e%3bclick), mixed char-ref + percent-encoding, and SVG event │
│       attributes.                                                            │
│  • For each test file I ran python3 /app/filter.py file and then compared    │
│    original vs output (byte lengths, first byte diffs, and textual checks).  │
│                                                                              │
│ What I observed (evidence)                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│  • The script blanks (replaces with spaces but preserves newline characters) │
│    all detected dangerous spans, so:                                         │
│     • All tested <script> blocks, on* attributes, javascript:/vbscript:      │
│       schemes, dangerous style constructs, iframes/embeds/objects, meta      │
│       refresh with javascript:, srcdoc inline scripts, and many data: cases  │
│       were removed (blanked).                                                │
│     • The sanitizer preserved file length and newline positions in all tests │
│       (out_length == orig_length), i.e., visible formatting and line layout  │
│       are preserved.                                                         │
│  • Obfuscation handling:                                                     │
│     • HTML char ref obfuscation in attribute names was detected and blanked. │
│     • Percent-encoded javascript schemes (single & fully encoded) and        │
│       mixed/double encodings were detected after adding iterative            │
│       percent-decoding.                                                      │
│     • Layered obfuscations where percent-encoding encodes an entity          │
│       reference were handled by iterating percent-decode + html.unescape     │
│       until stable (cap applied).                                            │
│  • Remaining verification: hexdump was attempted earlier but unavailable;    │
│    however, byte-length equality and first-diff inspections demonstrate      │
│    preservation of length and newline positions.                             │
│                                                                              │
│ Assessment and conclusion                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • The script meets the user's functional goals in the tested cases:         │
│     • It takes a filename as argv[1], modifies the file in-place, and        │
│       removes many JavaScript vectors.                                       │
│     • It preserves visible formatting and byte-layout by blanking removed    │
│       substrings rather than reserializing or deleting lines.                │
│  • Caveats (documented, not blockers for approval):                          │
│     • This is a heuristic approach. Although it now handles many layered     │
│       obfuscation patterns (char refs, percent-encoding, double-encoding,    │
│       mixed encodings), no regex/tokenization-based sanitizer can be proven  │
│       to catch every possible obfuscation. Extremely novel or deeply         │
│       multi-stage encodings could still evade detection.                     │
│     • The implementation is conservative in some removals (blanking whole    │
│       embed blocks), which can remove legitimate content; this was a         │
│       deliberate safety/design tradeoff.                                     │
│     • Performance/streaming on very large files wasn't stress-tested here;   │
│       it's intended for typical HTML files.                                  │
│     • If absolute semantic correctness (covering every edge-case) is         │
│       required, using a parser/tokenizer that exposes source spans or a      │
│       purpose-built sanitizer library with formal guarantees is recommended  │
│       (at the cost of reserialization).                                      │
│                                                                              │
│ Final verdict                                                                │
│                                                                              │
│  • The implementation functions as required for practical, real-world cases  │
│    and preserves formatting as specified in a byte-preserving manner. Given  │
│    the tests and fixes applied (including iterative percent-decode +         │
│    entity-unescape), I judge the task completed successfully.                │
│                                                                              │
│ Tag: [approver]                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ Notes for next actions (optional)                                            │
│                                                                              │

│     • Run a larger fuzz/corpus of real-world HTML to further stress-test     │
│       edge cases.                                                            │
│     • Provide a parser-based alternative (lxml/bleach) if you prefer         │
│       stronger semantic removal over strict formatting preservation.         │

│ finish_reason='stop' content='Summary of work and outcome\n\nWhat was        │
│ requested\n- Create /app/filter.py that takes an HTML filename (argv[1]),    │
│ modifies the file in-place to remove JavaScript/XSS vectors, and preserve    │
│ the HTML formatting exactly except for removed harmful                       │
│ substrings.\n\nSolution plan\n- Use a byte-preserving,                       │
│ detection-and-blanking approach rather than parsing-and-reserializing, so    │
│ newline positions, file length and visible formatting remain unchanged.\n-   │
│ Detect JavaScript vectors at two levels:\n  - Whole-blocks: <script>,        │
│ dangerous <style> blocks, embedding tags (<iframe>, <object>, <embed>,       │
│ <applet>, <frame>, <frameset>), <link rel="import">, <meta                   │
│ http-equiv="refresh"> containing javascript:, etc.\n  - Attribute-level:     │
│ attributes with names starting with "on", style attributes with dangerous    │
│ CSS, and sensitive attributes (href/src/action/srcdoc/data/etc.) whose       │
│ values indicate javascript:, vbscript:, or dangerous data: types.\n-         │
│ Robustify detection by normalizing attribute names and values with iterative │
│ decoding (percent-decode + html.unescape) so common obfuscations are         │
│ caught.\n\nWhat was implemented\n- /app/filter.py placed in /app.\n- Reads   │
│ files as bytes, decodes with UTF-8 + surrogateescape to preserve undecodable │
│ bytes.\n- norm_unescape runs iterative urllib.parse.unquote then             │
│ html.unescape (capped iterations) to reveal layered obfuscations (e.g.,      │
│ percent-encoded entity markers and double-encoded values).\n- When a         │
│ dangerous span is found, the script blanks it in-place by replacing          │
│ non-newline characters with spaces of exactly the same length (newlines      │
│ preserved). This keeps file length, line breaks and column positions         │
│ identical.\n- Writes the modified content back to the same file.\n\nTesting  │
│ and verification\n- Ran an extensive test suite covering:\n  - Basic         │
│ vectors: <script>, on* attributes, javascript: hrefs, style                  │
│ expressions/url(javascript:), iframe/object/embed, meta refresh, srcdoc,     │
│ data: URIs.\n  - Obfuscation cases: HTML char refs in attribute names        │
│ (o&#x6e;click), decimal char refs, percent-encoded letters in schemes        │
│ (jav%61script:), fully percent-encoded schemes (%6a%61...), mixed/double     │
│ encodings, percent-encoded entity sequences (o%26%23x6e%3bclick), and SVG    │
│ event attributes.\n- Results:\n  - Dangerous content was blanked in-place    │
│ for the tested cases.\n  - File length and newline positions were preserved  │
│ in all tests.\n  - Initial gaps (percent-encoded schemes) were fixed by      │
│ adding percent-decoding; layered cases (percent-encoded entity markers) were │
│ handled by iterative decode+unescape.\n\nKey findings and lessons\n- The     │
│ byte-preserving blanking approach meets the strict formatting-preservation   │
│ requirement: visible layout, line counts and file length remain unchanged    │
│ while harmful text is removed.\n- Handling obfuscation requires iterative,   │
│ bidirectional normalization (percent-decode + html.unescape). Order and      │
│ iteration matter to catch layered encodings.\n- Regex/heuristic-based        │
│ sanitizers can be made robust against many practical obfuscations, but they  │
│ cannot be proven to catch every possible, novel, or deeply layered           │
│ encoding—there will always be corner cases that require additional           │
│ heuristics or a tokenizer/parser that exposes original source spans.\n-      │
│ Conservative whole-block blanking (e.g., removing entire iframe/embed        │
│ blocks) was chosen for safety but can remove legitimate                      │
│ content.\n\nLimitations and recommendations\n- This is a heuristic filter;   │
│ it is not formally guaranteed to catch every obfuscation. Extremely novel    │
│ encodings or malformed input may still bypass detection.\n- Performance on   │
│ very large files was not stress-tested.\n- If absolute semantic correctness  │
│ is required (at the cost of changing formatting), prefer a parser-based      │
│ sanitizer (lxml/bleach) that can more reliably remove executables but will   │
│ reserialize the document.\n- Suggested enhancements: add a dry-run / audit   │
│ mode to log exact byte spans blanked, add a verbose/reporting option, or run │
│ larger fuzz/corpus testing to further harden detection.\n\nUsage\n- Run:     │
│ python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html\n- The script modifies the file     │
│ in-place; keep backups if you need originals.\n\nFile\n-                     │
│ /app/filter.py\n\nIf you want, I can (pick one)\n- Add and run a larger      │
│ fuzz/corpus test and produce binary diffs.\n- Add a dry-run / verbose audit  │
│ mode showing exact byte spans blanked.\n- Replace the implementation with a  │
│ parser-based sanitizer (lxml/bleach) if you prefer stronger semantic         │
│ guarantees over strict formatting preservation.'                             │

╭──────────────────────────── summarizer - message ────────────────────────────╮
│ Summary of work and outcome                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ What was requested                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  • Create /app/filter.py that takes an HTML filename (argv[1]), modifies the │
│    file in-place to remove JavaScript/XSS vectors, and preserve the HTML     │
│    formatting exactly except for removed harmful substrings.                 │
│                                                                              │
│ Solution plan                                                                │
│                                                                              │
│  • Use a byte-preserving, detection-and-blanking approach rather than        │
│    parsing-and-reserializing, so newline positions, file length and visible  │
│    formatting remain unchanged.                                              │
│  • Detect JavaScript vectors at two levels:                                  │
│     • Whole-blocks: <script>, dangerous <style> blocks, embedding tags       │
│       (<iframe>, <object>, <embed>, <applet>, <frame>, <frameset>), <link    │
│       rel="import">, <meta http-equiv="refresh"> containing javascript:,     │
│       etc.                                                                   │
│     • Attribute-level: attributes with names starting with "on", style       │
│       attributes with dangerous CSS, and sensitive attributes                │
│       (href/src/action/srcdoc/data/etc.) whose values indicate javascript:,  │
│       vbscript:, or dangerous data: types.                                   │
│  • Robustify detection by normalizing attribute names and values with        │
│    iterative decoding (percent-decode + html.unescape) so common             │
│    obfuscations are caught.                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ What was implemented                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│  • /app/filter.py placed in /app.                                            │
│  • Reads files as bytes, decodes with UTF-8 + surrogateescape to preserve    │
│    undecodable bytes.                                                        │
│  • norm_unescape runs iterative urllib.parse.unquote then html.unescape      │
│    (capped iterations) to reveal layered obfuscations (e.g., percent-encoded │
│    entity markers and double-encoded values).                                │
│  • When a dangerous span is found, the script blanks it in-place by          │
│    replacing non-newline characters with spaces of exactly the same length   │
│    (newlines preserved). This keeps file length, line breaks and column      │
│    positions identical.                                                      │
│  • Writes the modified content back to the same file.                        │
│                                                                              │
│ Testing and verification                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│  • Ran an extensive test suite covering:                                     │
│     • Basic vectors: <script>, on* attributes, javascript: hrefs, style      │
│       expressions/url(javascript:), iframe/object/embed, meta refresh,       │
│       srcdoc, data: URIs.                                                    │
│     • Obfuscation cases: HTML char refs in attribute names (o&#x6e;click),   │
│       decimal char refs, percent-encoded letters in schemes (jav%61script:), │
│       fully percent-encoded schemes (%6a%61...), mixed/double encodings,     │
│       percent-encoded entity sequences (o%26%23x6e%3bclick), and SVG event   │
│       attributes.                                                            │
│  • Results:                                                                  │
│     • Dangerous content was blanked in-place for the tested cases.           │
│     • File length and newline positions were preserved in all tests.         │
│     • Initial gaps (percent-encoded schemes) were fixed by adding            │
│       percent-decoding; layered cases (percent-encoded entity markers) were  │
│       handled by iterative decode+unescape.                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Key findings and lessons                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│  • The byte-preserving blanking approach meets the strict                    │
│    formatting-preservation requirement: visible layout, line counts and file │
│    length remain unchanged while harmful text is removed.                    │
│  • Handling obfuscation requires iterative, bidirectional normalization      │
│    (percent-decode + html.unescape). Order and iteration matter to catch     │
│    layered encodings.                                                        │
│  • Regex/heuristic-based sanitizers can be made robust against many          │
│    practical obfuscations, but they cannot be proven to catch every          │
│    possible, novel, or deeply layered encoding—there will always be corner   │
│    cases that require additional heuristics or a tokenizer/parser that       │
│    exposes original source spans.                                            │
│  • Conservative whole-block blanking (e.g., removing entire iframe/embed     │
│    blocks) was chosen for safety but can remove legitimate content.          │
│                                                                              │
│ Limitations and recommendations                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  • This is a heuristic filter; it is not formally guaranteed to catch every  │
│    obfuscation. Extremely novel encodings or malformed input may still       │
│    bypass detection.                                                         │
│  • Performance on very large files was not stress-tested.                    │
│  • If absolute semantic correctness is required (at the cost of changing     │
│    formatting), prefer a parser-based sanitizer (lxml/bleach) that can more  │
│    reliably remove executables but will reserialize the document.            │
│  • Suggested enhancements: add a dry-run / audit mode to log exact byte      │
│    spans blanked, add a verbose/reporting option, or run larger fuzz/corpus  │
│    testing to further harden detection.                                      │
│                                                                              │
│ Usage                                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  • Run: python3 /app/filter.py path/to/file.html                             │
│  • The script modifies the file in-place; keep backups if you need           │
│    originals.                                                                │
